# Sun and light audit, 18 October 2026 (phase S1)

Every published claim of a sunrise, sunset, twilight, golden or blue hour, darkness window, aurora window or light-direction window in the 14 trips under `legacy/` (the brief said 15; the repo has 14 trip folders, `peru` exists only under `research/trips/`), checked against `core/bin/sun.ts` (`core/src/sun`, longitude positive east, real IANA zone and daylight saving for the date). Town-centre coordinates are recorded per row. A difference over 5 minutes is WRONG. REVIEW rows are windows or labels wider than a strict rule, not computation errors.

## Conclusions

1. The bug is the longitude sign, and it is not confined to the four scripts. It reached three trips: Iceland (`sun-times.py`, `dark-times.py`), Norway (`sun-times.py`) and Patagonia (`logistics.md` section 6, from an untracked `sun-times-patagonia.py` copy). Patagonia's PLAN and `sun.md` had already been corrected by hand, which is why the page was right.
2. Error size is 8 minutes per degree of longitude: Bergen +43, Flam +56, Alesund +49, Andalsnes +62, Reine +105, Svolvaer +117, Senja +141, Tromso +152; Reykjavik -175, Myvatn -135, Calafate -578 (wraps past midnight).
3. The Rockies were not hit: `canadian-rockies-sept-2029/sun-times.py` still held the Norway places, but the plan computed its Rockies figures by calling the function with the right sign. All 72 checks pass. The script now holds the Rockies bases and the 2029 dates and reproduces the plan's Light table.
4. Every other trip (Dolomites, Switzerland, Chamonix, Costa Rica, Algonquin, Niagara, Vermont, Quebec, USA Southwest) used other tools (astral, PyEphem, USNO API, sunrise-sunset.org, own NOAA code) and agrees with the reference within about 3 minutes. New Hampshire has no light claims. The bug is isolated to the legacy NOAA copies.
5. Iceland's darkness durations and the "no darkness" rows were right; the clock windows were 135-175 minutes early. Separately, the Myvatn "10 September, 22:11-23:30, 1 h 19 m" claim used the 8 September row; the true 10 September window is 23:56-02:14 (2 h 18 m).
6. Non-bug WRONG rows: Dolomites transport.md labelled 21:30 as a sunset (true sunset 20:58, 21:30 is the end of colour); USA Southwest has rounded base-research sunsets (Springdale ~19:00 vs 19:14, Ouray 18:45 vs 18:52), loose sunrise ranges, a day-length difference of 28/34 minutes that is 22/27, and two elevation-corrected blue/golden-hour lines from sunrisesunset.io (left, the tool's own definition); Algonquin has six azimuth-rule windows (Echo Rock back-lit until 13:30 is 12:38 by a 60-degree rule, and so on) that depend on an assumed rule, left for the owner.
7. Norway builder line 350 shows Alesund sunset as "23:2x"; the reference is 23:33 on 1 Jul and 23:24 on 8 Jul, so it depends on the day; left unchanged.

## All claims

Difference is stated minus reference, in minutes. Rows marked WRONG in the Iceland, Norway and Patagonia sections show the value before correction; the files now carry the reference value.
